Hi... um, I came in because I just can't stop feeling like I'm a danger, honestly. And a huge disappointment. 

I had this... this one near-miss at work. I'm a nurse, and... well, it happened in room 4B. The AC in that room is always broken, so it’s constantly freezing in there, and maintenance keeps saying they’ll order a part, but they never do. My dad is an HVAC guy, actually, and he always says hospitals get completely ripped off by contractors. He wanted me to go into the business with him instead of doing college and nursing, but... I don't know. 

Sorry, I... I should have listened to him, probably. Because that near-miss... it just proves I'm not meant to be a nurse. I just keep thinking that everyone would be better off without me.

No, I... I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I just... I keep having these thoughts, you know? Like it would just be so much easier if I didn't wake up. Or if I could just... disappear. Then I wouldn't mess anything up again.

My brother hated it, he always wanted to be on the jet skis making a ton of noise. He actually crashed one once, completely totaled it. My parents were so mad, they made him pay for it out of his summer job money. He was working at this terrible pizza place... the uniform was this awful neon orange polo. But anyway, yeah... I just keep thinking about how nice it would be to just float away and not have to worry about making a mistake.

Um, it's been... I guess about a year and two months now. It really started right after I graduated high school, when I began taking college classes and working full-time at the clinic. 

I remember my high school graduation was outside on the football field, and it just poured rain the entire time. All of our graduation gowns got completely soaked, and they had this awful smell, kind of like wet dog? My friend Sarah actually slipped in the mud walking down the bleachers and totally sprained her ankle. She had to sit with her foot in a cooler of ice for her whole graduation party. Her mom had ordered these massive six-foot sub sandwiches from that deli on Main Street, but nobody even wanted to eat them because we were all just freezing and miserable. That deli actually closed down a few months ago, which is a shame because they had really good potato salad... 

But, um, yeah. Right after that, when I started doing the college classes and the full-time clinic work all at once... that's when this heavy feeling really set in.

Um... no, not really. I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, actually. Like, after class, I would go jogging, or I'd knit, or cook something nice, or just sit and read. I used to love reading those thick fantasy novels, the ones with the maps in the front? My roommate in the dorms, Jessica, she hated them. She said they were too nerdy. She was obsessed with reality TV, like, she would watch hours of those shows where people buy houses they haven't even seen. I don't get how anyone can do that, just buying a house without walking through it first. Houses have smells, you know? Like, what if it smells like mold or old cigarettes? My aunt bought a house once that smelled exactly like cats, and she had to rip up all the carpets. She found these gorgeous hardwood floors underneath, though, which was lucky. She stained them this dark cherry color... 

But anyway, yeah... now I don't do any of that stuff. I just... I come home from the clinic, and I just sit on my bed. Still in my scrubs. I just sit there and I can't even make myself care about doing anything else. It's just... blank.

I mean, like I said, I don't really do any of that stuff anymore. I just sit on my bed. There's not really anything I look forward to or... or enjoy. It's just exhausting. 

My bed is right by the window, actually, and sometimes I just watch the cars go by. There's this one guy who drives down our street every Tuesday at exactly 5:15 in this incredibly loud, bright yellow Mustang. It's so obnoxiously loud, it rattles the glass. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out and yelled at him once. She's this tiny little woman, must be in her eighties, and she just stood in the middle of the street waving her cane. She has this beautiful garden in her front yard, all these hydrangeas. They're pink, which means her soil is alkaline, I think? Or maybe acidic. I can never remember. My mom tried to grow hydrangeas once, but the deer just ate them all. We lived near these woods, and the deer would just come right up to the porch and eat everything... 

But, um, no. There isn't anything I enjoy doing right now. I just feel so tired all the time.

I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, actually. Like, after class, I would go jogging, or I'd knit, or cook something nice, or just sit and read. I used to love reading those thick fantasy novels, the ones with the maps in the front? My roommate in the dorms, Jessica, she hated them. She said they were too nerdy. She was obsessed with reality TV, like, she would watch hours of those shows where people buy houses they haven't even seen. I don't get how anyone can do that, just buying a house without walking through it first. Houses have smells, you know? Like, what if it smells like mold or old cigarettes? My aunt bought a house once that smelled exactly like cats, and she had to rip up all the carpets. She found these gorgeous hardwood floors underneath, though, which was lucky. She stained them this dark cherry color... \n\nBut anyway, yeah... now I don't do any of that stuff. I just... I come home from the clinic, and I just sit on my bed. Still in my scrubs. I just sit there and I can't even make myself care about doing anything else. It's just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said, I don't really do any of that stuff anymore. I just sit on my bed. There's not really anything I look forward to or... or enjoy. It's just exhausting. \n\nMy bed is right by the window, actually, and sometimes I just watch the cars go by. There's this one guy who drives down our street every Tuesday at exactly 5:15 in this incredibly loud, bright yellow Mustang. It's so obnoxiously loud, it rattles the glass. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out and yelled at him once. She's this tiny little woman, must be in her eighties, and she just stood in the middle of the street waving her cane. She has this beautiful garden in her front yard, all these hydrangeas. They're pink, which means her soil is alkaline, I think? Or maybe acidic. I can never remember. My mom tried to grow hydrangeas once, but the deer just ate them all. We lived near these woods, and the deer would just come right up to the porch and eat everything... \n\nBut, um, no. There isn't anything I enjoy doing right now. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah, I guess so. My work pants have been getting really loose lately, and I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. I just... I forget to eat until really late in the day, and then I'll just force down some crackers or, like, black coffee. 

The coffee at the clinic is actually terrible. It's that cheap powdered stuff that comes in those massive plastic tubs? The head nurse, Brenda, she buys it in bulk from Costco. She's always talking about her Costco trips. She bought this huge inflatable kayak there last month, even though she doesn't even know how to swim. She said she's going to take it out on the lake this summer, but I don't know, that seems really dangerous to me. The lake gets so crowded with those big speedboats, and people are always drinking out there. My uncle actually got a ticket once for driving his boat too fast near the dock. He was so mad, he tried to argue with the water police, which is just a terrible idea. He's always arguing with people, though. He even argued with the guy at the hardware store about the price of nails... 

But, um, yeah, I haven't been eating much. Just the crackers and coffee mostly.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, yeah, I guess I have been moving slower. Some of the other nurses at the clinic have actually asked if I'm tired because I'm just dragging when I go to get supplies from the stockroom. And sometimes it takes me a few seconds to even process what they're saying and answer simple questions. 

The stockroom is always freezing, by the way. I don't know why they keep the AC cranked up so high in there. We have this one supply tech, Gary, and he wears a winter beanie and a puffy jacket in there in the middle of July. He's always complaining about the cold. He said he was going to bring in a space heater, but Brenda—the head nurse I told you about—she absolutely lost it. She said it was a fire hazard and threatened to write him up. She takes the fire code very seriously. Like, she made us all practice using the fire extinguishers last month. We had to go out to the back parking lot and spray them at this metal trash can. It was actually kind of fun, the foam goes everywhere. It got all over Dr. Miller's shoes. He wears these really expensive leather loafers, and he was so upset. He spent like twenty minutes in the breakroom trying to wipe them off with paper towels...

But yeah, I'm just moving really slow lately. It's like I'm walking through water or something.

I just sit on my bed. There's not really anything I look forward to or... or enjoy. It's just exhausting. \n\nMy bed is right by the window, actually, and sometimes I just watch the cars go by. She said she's going to take it out on the lake this summer, but I don't know, that seems really dangerous to me. He was so mad, he tried to argue with the water police, which is just a terrible idea. He's always arguing with people, though. He even argued with the guy at the hardware store about the price of nails... \n\nBut, um, yeah, I haven't been eating much. Just the crackers and coffee mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I have been moving slower. Some of the other nurses at the clinic have actually asked if I'm tired because I'm just dragging when I go to get supplies from the stockroom. I don't know why they keep the AC cranked up so high in there. We have this one supply tech, Gary, and he wears a winter beanie and a puffy jacket in there in the middle of July. Um, no, not really anxious. I don't feel on edge or anything like that. Just... just tired and empty, mostly. 

Though speaking of edges, the edge of the counter in the breakroom is completely chipped. It's been like that for months, and someone put this bright blue painter's tape over it so nobody would scratch themselves. It looks awful. Brenda keeps saying she's going to put in a work order for maintenance to fix it, but maintenance takes forever. The maintenance guy, Carl, he's always wandering around with this huge ring of keys that jingle so loudly you can hear him coming from down the hall. He has this massive dog, a Great Dane, I think? He brings pictures of it to show everyone. The dog's name is Barnaby, and it sleeps on his couch. He said it actually broke the springs on his old couch because it's so heavy. I can't imagine having a dog that big in an apartment. I used to have a hamster when I was little, named Sir Fluffington. He used to run on his wheel all night long, and it squeaked so much my dad had to take it out of the cage so we could sleep...

But, um, no. I don't feel anxious. Just really drained.

Um, no, I don't think so. I haven't really had any good days or, like, periods where I felt better in a long time. It just feels like one long blur, honestly. 

Which is weird because time usually goes by so fast. Like, my cousin just had her baby, and I swear she just announced she was pregnant, like, yesterday. She had a little girl, and she named her Hazel. I think Hazel is such a cute name. It reminds me of those hazelnut coffees they sell at this little cafe down the street from the clinic. The barista there, his name is Leo, he has this crazy curly hair and he always wears these bright yellow Converse. He told me once he buys them online because you can't find that exact shade of yellow in stores anymore. He's super into shoes. He actually went to a sneaker convention last weekend in the city. He said there were thousands of people there just buying and trading shoes, which I didn't even know was a thing. I just wear my white nursing clogs every day, and they are so uncomfortable. The soles are completely worn down on the left side because I guess I walk kind of crooked? I tried putting those gel inserts in them, but they just slide around when I walk and it feels really weird on my toes...

But, yeah, to answer your question... no, I haven't really felt any better. It's just been pretty much the same.

Um, well, I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I do keep having these thoughts. Like, maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up, or if I could just disappear so I wouldn't mess anything up again. 

Speaking of messing things up, I completely ruined a batch of cookies I tried to bake last week. I was trying to make those chocolate chip ones with the sea salt on top? I saw a video for them on my phone, and the girl making them had this gorgeous kitchen with marble countertops and a massive island. She was using this stand mixer that was like, a really pretty mint green color. I've always wanted a stand mixer, but they're so expensive, and my apartment kitchen is tiny anyway. I barely have enough counter space for my toaster and my coffee maker. My coffee maker is actually starting to make this weird sputtering noise when it brews. I think the water line is clogged because the water where I live is really hard. My roommate, Sarah, bought this special descaling liquid for it, but she left it sitting on the counter right next to the dish soap, and they're both blue. I almost used it to wash a pan the other day. Sarah would have been so mad. She's really particular about her pans. She has this cast iron skillet that she refuses to let anyone else wash because she says we'll ruin the seasoning. She cooks everything in it, even eggs, which I think is kind of gross because it always smells a little bit like whatever she cooked last...

But um, yeah, just... thoughts about disappearing. No actual plans or anything.

She was obsessed with reality TV, like, she would watch hours of those shows where people buy houses they haven't even seen. My aunt bought a house once that smelled exactly like cats, and she had to rip up all the carpets. She found these gorgeous hardwood floors underneath, though, which was lucky. She stained them this dark cherry color... \n\nBut anyway, yeah... now I don't do any of that stuff. I just... I come home from the clinic, and I just sit on my bed. Still in my scrubs. I just sit there and I can't even make myself care about doing anything else. It's just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said, I don't really do any of that stuff anymore. I just sit on my bed. There's not really anything I look forward to or... or enjoy. It's just exhausting. \n\nMy bed is right by the window, actually, and sometimes I just watch the cars go by. There's this one guy who drives down our street every Tuesday at exactly 5:15 in this incredibly loud, bright yellow Mustang. It's so obnoxiously loud, it rattles the glass. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out and yelled at him once. She's this tiny little woman, must be in her eighties, and she just stood in the middle of the street waving her cane. She has this beautiful garden in her front yard, all these hydrangeas. They're pink, which means her soil is alkaline, I think? Or maybe acidic. I can never remember. My mom tried to grow hydrangeas once, but the deer just ate them all. We lived near these woods, and the deer would just come right up to the porch and eat everything... \n\nBut, um, no. There isn't anything I enjoy doing right now. I just feel so tired all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. My work pants have been getting really loose lately, and I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. I just... I forget to eat until really late in the day, and then I'll just force down some crackers or, like, black coffee. \n\nThe coffee at the clinic is actually terrible. It's that cheap powdered stuff that comes in those massive plastic tubs? The head nurse, Brenda, she buys it in bulk from Costco. She's always talking about her Costco trips. She bought this huge inflatable kayak there last month, even though she doesn't even know how to swim. She said she's going to take it out on the lake this summer, but I don't know, that seems really dangerous to me. The lake gets so crowded with those big speedboats, and people are always drinking out there. My uncle actually got a ticket once for driving his boat too fast near the dock. He was so mad, he tried to argue with the water police, which is just a terrible idea. He's always arguing with people, though. He even argued with the guy at the hardware store about the price of nails... \n\nBut, um, yeah, I haven't been eating much. Just the crackers and coffee mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I have been moving slower. Some of the other nurses at the clinic have actually asked if I'm tired because I'm just dragging when I go to get supplies from the stockroom. And sometimes it takes me a few seconds to even process what they're saying and answer simple questions. \n\nThe stockroom is always freezing, by the way. I don't know why they keep the AC cranked up so high in there. We have this one supply tech, Gary, and he wears a winter beanie and a puffy jacket in there in the middle of July. Just... just tired and empty, mostly. \n\nThough speaking of edges, the edge of the counter in the breakroom is completely chipped. It's been like that for months, and someone put this bright blue painter's tape over it so nobody would scratch themselves. It looks awful. Brenda keeps saying she's going to put in a work order for maintenance to fix it, but maintenance takes forever. The maintenance guy, Carl, he's always wandering around with this huge ring of keys that jingle so loudly you can hear him coming from down the hall. He has this massive dog, a Great Dane, I think? He brings pictures of it to show everyone. The dog's name is Barnaby, and it sleeps on his couch. He said it actually broke the springs on his old couch because it's so heavy. I can't imagine having a dog that big in an apartment. I used to have a hamster when I was little, named Sir Fluffington. He used to run on his wheel all night long, and it squeaked so much my dad had to take it out of the cage so we could sleep...\n\nBut, um, no. I don't feel anxious. Just really drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling slightly better over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I barely have enough counter space for my toaster and my coffee maker. My coffee maker is actually starting to make this weird sputtering noise when it brews. I was trying to make those chocolate chip ones with the sea salt on top? I barely have enough counter space for my toaster and my coffee maker. My coffee maker is actually starting to make this weird sputtering noise when it brews. Um, not really. I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, you know? Like after my nursing classes, I would go jogging, or I'd knit, or cook, or read a book. But now... I just don't care. I'll get home and just sit on my bed in my scrubs and I can't make myself care about any of it. 

Which is a shame because I actually have this really nice yarn I bought a while ago. It's this chunky, soft wool, dyed in these pretty jewel tones—like deep purples and emerald greens. I bought it from this little independent yarn shop that opened up near the hospital. The lady who owns it, Mrs. Higgins, is so sweet. She always has a pot of tea going and she knits these tiny little sweaters for premature babies in the NICU. She told me she uses special, ultra-soft cotton for them so it doesn't irritate their skin. My friend Jenna works in the NICU, and she says those tiny sweaters are the cutest things she's ever seen. Jenna just got engaged, actually. Her fiancé proposed on a hot air balloon ride, which sounds terrifying to me. I hate heights. I went on a Ferris wheel once when I was ten and I cried the entire time until the operator let us off early. The guy running the ride was eating a funnel cake and got powdered sugar all over the control panel...

But, yeah. No joy in anything, really. Just sitting in my scrubs, doing nothing.

Her mom had ordered these massive six-foot sub sandwiches from that deli on Main Street, but nobody even wanted to eat them because we were all just freezing and miserable. That deli actually closed down a few months ago, which is a shame because they had really good potato salad... \n\nBut, um, yeah. Right after that, when I started doing the college classes and the full-time clinic work all at once... that's when this heavy feeling really set in.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, actually. Like, after class, I would go jogging, or I'd knit, or cook something nice, or just sit and read. I used to love reading those thick fantasy novels, the ones with the maps in the front? My roommate in the dorms, Jessica, she hated them. She said they were too nerdy. She was obsessed with reality TV, like, she would watch hours of those shows where people buy houses they haven't even seen. I don't get how anyone can do that, just buying a house without walking through it first. Houses have smells, you know? Like, what if it smells like mold or old cigarettes? My aunt bought a house once that smelled exactly like cats, and she had to rip up all the carpets. She found these gorgeous hardwood floors underneath, though, which was lucky. She stained them this dark cherry color... \n\nBut anyway, yeah... now I don't do any of that stuff. I just... I come home from the clinic, and I just sit on my bed. Still in my scrubs. I just sit there and I can't even make myself care about doing anything else. It's just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said, I don't really do any of that stuff anymore. I just sit on my bed. There's not really anything I look forward to or... or enjoy. It's just exhausting. \n\nMy bed is right by the window, actually, and sometimes I just watch the cars go by. There's this one guy who drives down our street every Tuesday at exactly 5:15 in this incredibly loud, bright yellow Mustang. It's so obnoxiously loud, it rattles the glass. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out and yelled at him once. She's this tiny little woman, must be in her eighties, and she just stood in the middle of the street waving her cane. She has this beautiful garden in her front yard, all these hydrangeas. They're pink, which means her soil is alkaline, I think? Or maybe acidic. I can never remember. My mom tried to grow hydrangeas once, but the deer just ate them all. We lived near these woods, and the deer would just come right up to the porch and eat everything... \n\nBut, um, no. There isn't anything I enjoy doing right now. I just feel so tired all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. My work pants have been getting really loose lately, and I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. I just... I forget to eat until really late in the day, and then I'll just force down some crackers or, like, black coffee. \n\nThe coffee at the clinic is actually terrible. It's that cheap powdered stuff that comes in those massive plastic tubs? The head nurse, Brenda, she buys it in bulk from Costco. She's always talking about her Costco trips. She bought this huge inflatable kayak there last month, even though she doesn't even know how to swim. She said she's going to take it out on the lake this summer, but I don't know, that seems really dangerous to me. The lake gets so crowded with those big speedboats, and people are always drinking out there. My uncle actually got a ticket once for driving his boat too fast near the dock. He was so mad, he tried to argue with the water police, which is just a terrible idea. He's always arguing with people, though. He even argued with the guy at the hardware store about the price of nails... \n\nBut, um, yeah, I haven't been eating much. Just the crackers and coffee mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I have been moving slower. Some of the other nurses at the clinic have actually asked if I'm tired because I'm just dragging when I go to get supplies from the stockroom. And sometimes it takes me a few seconds to even process what they're saying and answer simple questions. \n\nThe stockroom is always freezing, by the way. I don't know why they keep the AC cranked up so high in there. We have this one supply tech, Gary, and he wears a winter beanie and a puffy jacket in there in the middle of July. He's always complaining about the cold. He said he was going to bring in a space heater, but Brenda—the head nurse I told you about—she absolutely lost it. She said it was a fire hazard and threatened to write him up. She takes the fire code very seriously. Like, she made us all practice using the fire extinguishers last month. We had to go out to the back parking lot and spray them at this metal trash can. It was actually kind of fun, the foam goes everywhere. It got all over Dr. Miller's shoes. He wears these really expensive leather loafers, and he was so upset. He spent like twenty minutes in the breakroom trying to wipe them off with paper towels...\n\nBut yeah, I'm just moving really slow lately. It's like I'm walking through water or something.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really anxious. I don't feel on edge or anything like that. Just... just tired and empty, mostly. \n\nThough speaking of edges, the edge of the counter in the breakroom is completely chipped. It's been like that for months, and someone put this bright blue painter's tape over it so nobody would scratch themselves. It looks awful. Brenda keeps saying she's going to put in a work order for maintenance to fix it, but maintenance takes forever. The maintenance guy, Carl, he's always wandering around with this huge ring of keys that jingle so loudly you can hear him coming from down the hall. He has this massive dog, a Great Dane, I think? He brings pictures of it to show everyone. The dog's name is Barnaby, and it sleeps on his couch. He said it actually broke the springs on his old couch because it's so heavy. I can't imagine having a dog that big in an apartment. I used to have a hamster when I was little, named Sir Fluffington. He used to run on his wheel all night long, and it squeaked so much my dad had to take it out of the cage so we could sleep...\n\nBut, um, no. I don't feel anxious. Just really drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling slightly better over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I haven't really had any good days or, like, periods where I felt better in a long time. It just feels like one long blur, honestly. \n\nWhich is weird because time usually goes by so fast. Like, my cousin just had her baby, and I swear she just announced she was pregnant, like, yesterday. She had a little girl, and she named her Hazel. I think Hazel is such a cute name. It reminds me of those hazelnut coffees they sell at this little cafe down the street from the clinic. The barista there, his name is Leo, he has this crazy curly hair and he always wears these bright yellow Converse. He told me once he buys them online because you can't find that exact shade of yellow in stores anymore. He's super into shoes. He actually went to a sneaker convention last weekend in the city. He said there were thousands of people there just buying and trading shoes, which I didn't even know was a thing. I just wear my white nursing clogs every day, and they are so uncomfortable. The soles are completely worn down on the left side because I guess I walk kind of crooked? I tried putting those gel inserts in them, but they just slide around when I walk and it feels really weird on my toes...\n\nBut, yeah, to answer your question... no, I haven't really felt any better. It's just been pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I was trying to make those chocolate chip ones with the sea salt on top? I saw a video for them on my phone, and the girl making them had this gorgeous kitchen with marble countertops and a massive island. No actual plans or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: Um, not really. I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, you know? Um, well, like I was just saying... I used to do a lot of things. After my nursing classes, I'd go jogging to clear my head, or I'd knit, or cook, or read a book. But now, I just don't care about any of it. I literally just sit on my bed in my scrubs and I can't make myself care. 

Speaking of scrubs, the hospital just changed our uniform policy. We used to be able to wear any color we wanted, as long as it was solid, but now they're making all the nurses wear this specific shade of navy blue. It's so boring. And the brand they want us to buy from is so expensive. My friend Mark, he's a tech on the fourth floor, he found a loophole and bought this off-brand navy that's like, half a shade lighter, and he hasn't gotten caught yet. He's always trying to find ways around the rules. Like, he parks in the visitor lot instead of the employee garage because it's closer to the entrance, and he just keeps his badge hidden when he walks in. The employee garage is a nightmare anyway. The spaces are so tiny. I drive a really old Honda Civic, and even I have trouble fitting in there. My car's actually been making a weird rattling noise when I go over 40 miles an hour. I think it might be the muffler? My dad said he'd look at it, but he lives two hours away and I hate driving on the highway. The last time I drove on the highway, I saw this huge truck carrying a bunch of logs, and it totally reminded me of that one scary movie where the logs fall off and smash into the car behind it. I was so paranoid the whole time, I stayed like, three lanes away from it...

But um, yeah. Jogging, knitting, cooking... I just don't care about doing any of it anymore.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi... um, I came in because I just can't stop feeling like I'm a danger, honestly. And a huge disappointment. \n\nI had this... this one near-miss at work. I'm a nurse, and... well, it happened in room 4B. The AC in that room is always broken, so it’s constantly freezing in there, and maintenance keeps saying they’ll order a part, but they never do. My dad is an HVAC guy, actually, and he always says hospitals get completely ripped off by contractors. He wanted me to go into the business with him instead of doing college and nursing, but... I don't know. \n\nSorry, I... I should have listened to him, probably. Because that near-miss... it just proves I'm not meant to be a nurse. I just keep thinking that everyone would be better off without me.\n\nDoctor: Have you had thoughts of harming yourself?\n\nPatient: No, I... I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But I just... I keep having these thoughts, you know? Like it would just be so much easier if I didn't wake up. Or if I could just... disappear. Then I wouldn't mess anything up again. \n\nIt's like when I was a kid, we used to go camping up by the lake, and I would just float in the water for hours. It was so quiet under there. My brother hated it, he always wanted to be on the jet skis making a ton of noise. He actually crashed one once, completely totaled it. My parents were so mad, they made him pay for it out of his summer job money. He was working at this terrible pizza place... the uniform was this awful neon orange polo. But anyway, yeah... I just keep thinking about how nice it would be to just float away and not have to worry about making a mistake.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling like you're a disappointment and a danger to others?\n\nPatient: Um, it's been... I guess about a year and two months now. It really started right after I graduated high school, when I began taking college classes and working full-time at the clinic. \n\nI remember my high school graduation was outside on the football field, and it just poured rain the entire time. All of our graduation gowns got completely soaked, and they had this awful smell, kind of like wet dog? My friend Sarah actually slipped in the mud walking down the bleachers and totally sprained her ankle. She had to sit with her foot in a cooler of ice for her whole graduation party. Her mom had ordered these massive six-foot sub sandwiches from that deli on Main Street, but nobody even wanted to eat them because we were all just freezing and miserable. That deli actually closed down a few months ago, which is a shame because they had really good potato salad... \n\nBut, um, yeah. Right after that, when I started doing the college classes and the full-time clinic work all at once... that's when this heavy feeling really set in.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, actually. Like, after class, I would go jogging, or I'd knit, or cook something nice, or just sit and read. I used to love reading those thick fantasy novels, the ones with the maps in the front? My roommate in the dorms, Jessica, she hated them. She said they were too nerdy. She was obsessed with reality TV, like, she would watch hours of those shows where people buy houses they haven't even seen. I don't get how anyone can do that, just buying a house without walking through it first. Houses have smells, you know? Like, what if it smells like mold or old cigarettes? My aunt bought a house once that smelled exactly like cats, and she had to rip up all the carpets. She found these gorgeous hardwood floors underneath, though, which was lucky. She stained them this dark cherry color... \n\nBut anyway, yeah... now I don't do any of that stuff. I just... I come home from the clinic, and I just sit on my bed. Still in my scrubs. I just sit there and I can't even make myself care about doing anything else. It's just... blank.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still enjoy or find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I mean, like I said, I don't really do any of that stuff anymore. I just sit on my bed. There's not really anything I look forward to or... or enjoy. It's just exhausting. \n\nMy bed is right by the window, actually, and sometimes I just watch the cars go by. There's this one guy who drives down our street every Tuesday at exactly 5:15 in this incredibly loud, bright yellow Mustang. It's so obnoxiously loud, it rattles the glass. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she actually went out and yelled at him once. She's this tiny little woman, must be in her eighties, and she just stood in the middle of the street waving her cane. She has this beautiful garden in her front yard, all these hydrangeas. They're pink, which means her soil is alkaline, I think? Or maybe acidic. I can never remember. My mom tried to grow hydrangeas once, but the deer just ate them all. We lived near these woods, and the deer would just come right up to the porch and eat everything... \n\nBut, um, no. There isn't anything I enjoy doing right now. I just feel so tired all the time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess so. My work pants have been getting really loose lately, and I'm definitely not trying to lose weight or anything. I just... I forget to eat until really late in the day, and then I'll just force down some crackers or, like, black coffee. \n\nThe coffee at the clinic is actually terrible. It's that cheap powdered stuff that comes in those massive plastic tubs? The head nurse, Brenda, she buys it in bulk from Costco. She's always talking about her Costco trips. She bought this huge inflatable kayak there last month, even though she doesn't even know how to swim. She said she's going to take it out on the lake this summer, but I don't know, that seems really dangerous to me. The lake gets so crowded with those big speedboats, and people are always drinking out there. My uncle actually got a ticket once for driving his boat too fast near the dock. He was so mad, he tried to argue with the water police, which is just a terrible idea. He's always arguing with people, though. He even argued with the guy at the hardware store about the price of nails... \n\nBut, um, yeah, I haven't been eating much. Just the crackers and coffee mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I have been moving slower. Some of the other nurses at the clinic have actually asked if I'm tired because I'm just dragging when I go to get supplies from the stockroom. And sometimes it takes me a few seconds to even process what they're saying and answer simple questions. \n\nThe stockroom is always freezing, by the way. I don't know why they keep the AC cranked up so high in there. We have this one supply tech, Gary, and he wears a winter beanie and a puffy jacket in there in the middle of July. He's always complaining about the cold. He said he was going to bring in a space heater, but Brenda—the head nurse I told you about—she absolutely lost it. She said it was a fire hazard and threatened to write him up. She takes the fire code very seriously. Like, she made us all practice using the fire extinguishers last month. We had to go out to the back parking lot and spray them at this metal trash can. It was actually kind of fun, the foam goes everywhere. It got all over Dr. Miller's shoes. He wears these really expensive leather loafers, and he was so upset. He spent like twenty minutes in the breakroom trying to wipe them off with paper towels...\n\nBut yeah, I'm just moving really slow lately. It's like I'm walking through water or something.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really anxious. I don't feel on edge or anything like that. Just... just tired and empty, mostly. \n\nThough speaking of edges, the edge of the counter in the breakroom is completely chipped. It's been like that for months, and someone put this bright blue painter's tape over it so nobody would scratch themselves. It looks awful. Brenda keeps saying she's going to put in a work order for maintenance to fix it, but maintenance takes forever. The maintenance guy, Carl, he's always wandering around with this huge ring of keys that jingle so loudly you can hear him coming from down the hall. He has this massive dog, a Great Dane, I think? He brings pictures of it to show everyone. The dog's name is Barnaby, and it sleeps on his couch. He said it actually broke the springs on his old couch because it's so heavy. I can't imagine having a dog that big in an apartment. I used to have a hamster when I was little, named Sir Fluffington. He used to run on his wheel all night long, and it squeaked so much my dad had to take it out of the cage so we could sleep...\n\nBut, um, no. I don't feel anxious. Just really drained.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling slightly better over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I haven't really had any good days or, like, periods where I felt better in a long time. It just feels like one long blur, honestly. \n\nWhich is weird because time usually goes by so fast. Like, my cousin just had her baby, and I swear she just announced she was pregnant, like, yesterday. She had a little girl, and she named her Hazel. I think Hazel is such a cute name. It reminds me of those hazelnut coffees they sell at this little cafe down the street from the clinic. The barista there, his name is Leo, he has this crazy curly hair and he always wears these bright yellow Converse. He told me once he buys them online because you can't find that exact shade of yellow in stores anymore. He's super into shoes. He actually went to a sneaker convention last weekend in the city. He said there were thousands of people there just buying and trading shoes, which I didn't even know was a thing. I just wear my white nursing clogs every day, and they are so uncomfortable. The soles are completely worn down on the left side because I guess I walk kind of crooked? I tried putting those gel inserts in them, but they just slide around when I walk and it feels really weird on my toes...\n\nBut, yeah, to answer your question... no, I haven't really felt any better. It's just been pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever wished you were dead?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I do keep having these thoughts. Like, maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up, or if I could just disappear so I wouldn't mess anything up again. \n\nSpeaking of messing things up, I completely ruined a batch of cookies I tried to bake last week. I was trying to make those chocolate chip ones with the sea salt on top? I saw a video for them on my phone, and the girl making them had this gorgeous kitchen with marble countertops and a massive island. She was using this stand mixer that was like, a really pretty mint green color. I've always wanted a stand mixer, but they're so expensive, and my apartment kitchen is tiny anyway. I barely have enough counter space for my toaster and my coffee maker. My coffee maker is actually starting to make this weird sputtering noise when it brews. I think the water line is clogged because the water where I live is really hard. My roommate, Sarah, bought this special descaling liquid for it, but she left it sitting on the counter right next to the dish soap, and they're both blue. I almost used it to wash a pan the other day. Sarah would have been so mad. She's really particular about her pans. She has this cast iron skillet that she refuses to let anyone else wash because she says we'll ruin the seasoning. She cooks everything in it, even eggs, which I think is kind of gross because it always smells a little bit like whatever she cooked last...\n\nBut um, yeah, just... thoughts about disappearing. No actual plans or anything.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you a little bit of joy?\n\nPatient: Um, not really. I used to do a lot of things to clear my head, you know? Like after my nursing classes, I would go jogging, or I'd knit, or cook, or read a book. But now... I just don't care. I'll get home and just sit on my bed in my scrubs and I can't make myself care about any of it. \n\nWhich is a shame because I actually have this really nice yarn I bought a while ago. It's this chunky, soft wool, dyed in these pretty jewel tones—like deep purples and emerald greens. I bought it from this little independent yarn shop that opened up near the hospital. The lady who owns it, Mrs. Higgins, is so sweet. She always has a pot of tea going and she knits these tiny little sweaters for premature babies in the NICU. She told me she uses special, ultra-soft cotton for them so it doesn't irritate their skin. My friend Jenna works in the NICU, and she says those tiny sweaters are the cutest things she's ever seen. Jenna just got engaged, actually. Her fiancé proposed on a hot air balloon ride, which sounds terrifying to me. I hate heights. I went on a Ferris wheel once when I was ten and I cried the entire time until the operator let us off early. The guy running the ride was eating a funnel cake and got powdered sugar all over the control panel...\n\nBut, yeah. No joy in anything, really. Just sitting in my scrubs, doing nothing.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now find you no longer take pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I was just saying... I used to do a lot of things. After my nursing classes, I'd go jogging to clear my head, or I'd knit, or cook, or read a book. But now, I just don't care about any of it. I literally just sit on my bed in my scrubs and I can't make myself care. \n\nSpeaking of scrubs, the hospital just changed our uniform policy. We used to be able to wear any color we wanted, as long as it was solid, but now they're making all the nurses wear this specific shade of navy blue. It's so boring. And the brand they want us to buy from is so expensive. My friend Mark, he's a tech on the fourth floor, he found a loophole and bought this off-brand navy that's like, half a shade lighter, and he hasn't gotten caught yet. He's always trying to find ways around the rules. Like, he parks in the visitor lot instead of the employee garage because it's closer to the entrance, and he just keeps his badge hidden when he walks in. The employee garage is a nightmare anyway. The spaces are so tiny. I drive a really old Honda Civic, and even I have trouble fitting in there. My car's actually been making a weird rattling noise when I go over 40 miles an hour. I think it might be the muffler? My dad said he'd look at it, but he lives two hours away and I hate driving on the highway. The last time I drove on the highway, I saw this huge truck carrying a bunch of logs, and it totally reminded me of that one scary movie where the logs fall off and smash into the car behind it. I was so paranoid the whole time, I stayed like, three lanes away from it...\n\nBut um, yeah. Jogging, knitting, cooking... I just don't care about doing any of it anymore.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Um, well, like I said, not really. I mean, I used to enjoy a lot of things. Jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... those were my go-to things to clear my head after my nursing classes. But now, it's just nothing. I come home, sit on my bed in my scrubs, and I just can't make myself care about doing any of them. 

It's weird because I used to love reading. I had this huge collection of paperback thrillers. I'd get them from this used bookstore downtown. The smell of that place was amazing, like old paper and dust and coffee. The guy who ran it, Mr. Abernathy, he had this huge orange cat named Marmalade that would just sleep on the stacks of books. Marmalade was so fat, he looked like a pumpkin. My roommate Sarah is actually allergic to cats, which is a bummer because I'd love to get one. But she breaks out in hives if she even looks at a cat. She had to go to the ER once because her throat started closing up after she visited a friend who had a Persian. ERs are so chaotic. I did a clinical rotation in one last semester and it was non-stop. There was this one guy who came in with a fishing hook stuck in his thumb. He was totally calm about it, though. Said he was trying to catch a largemouth bass and the fish fought back. He was wearing this really bright neon green fishing shirt that was practically blinding under the fluorescent hospital lights. Those lights always give me a headache if I stare at them too long. 

But um... yeah. To answer your question, no. I don't really find pleasure in anything right now. Just sitting on my bed in my scrubs.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no. Like I've been saying... I really don't. I used to. I used to do a lot of things. Jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... those were my go-to things to clear my head after my nursing classes. But now, it's just nothing. I come home, sit on my bed in my scrubs, and I just can't make myself care about doing any of them. Even just a little bit. 

I don't even care about cooking anymore, and I used to make these amazing homemade pizzas. I'd make the dough from scratch and let it rise overnight. The secret is using a really hot pizza stone in the oven. My mom gave me hers when I moved into my apartment. She got it for her wedding back in the nineties. My parents had this huge wedding with like, three hundred guests, which sounds exhausting. They had a live band that played all these cheesy eighties covers. My dad actually got up and sang "Don't Stop Believin'" with them. He thinks he's a great singer, but he's totally tone-deaf. He always sings in the shower and it echoes through the whole house. When I was a kid, I used to think there was a ghost in the bathroom making those noises. I was terrified of ghosts. I wouldn't sleep without a nightlight until I was like, twelve. 

But um... no. I don't care about the pizza or the jogging or any of it. Just sitting on my bed.

Um, no, not really. It's just been pretty much the same this whole time. I mean, this has been going on for about a year and two months now. It started right after I graduated high school and started taking my college classes and working full-time at the clinic. 

High school graduation feels like it was a million years ago, honestly. My grandma flew in from Florida for it, and she brought me this huge bouquet of yellow roses. She loves yellow roses. She grows them in her backyard down there. The weather in Florida is so crazy, though. Sarah has this perfectly straight, shiny hair, and she doesn't even do anything to it. She just wakes up and it looks great. It's so unfair. She uses this weird coconut shampoo that smells like a tropical vacation, which always makes me crave a piña colada. We actually tried making virgin piña coladas in our blender once, but the blender is super cheap and it didn't crush the ice right, so it was just like... coconut milk with giant chunks of ice floating in it. We ended up just throwing it out. 

But um, anyway. No, I haven't really felt better at all. It's just been this way for a year and two months, since I started all my classes and clinic work.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no... I don't really look forward to anything. Like I said before, I used to do stuff to clear my head after my nursing classes. I'd go jogging, or knit, or cook, or read... but now I just sit on my bed in my scrubs and I can't make myself care about any of it. 

I actually bought this really pretty blue yarn a while ago to knit a scarf. It was like a deep navy blue, almost the color of the ocean at night. I went to the beach once at night when I was little, on a family vacation to North Carolina. The sand was so cold, and my brother kept trying to catch these little ghost crabs that were scurrying around. He actually got pinched by one and cried for like an hour. My brother is such a baby when it comes to pain. He got a paper cut last week and acted like he needed stitches. He's studying accounting, which sounds so boring to me. Just looking at spreadsheets all day. I don't know how people sit at desks for eight hours staring at numbers. I need to be moving around, which is why I thought nursing would be good, but now... 

Now I just don't want to do anything. Just sit on my bed. So no, nothing I really enjoy.

Um, yeah... pretty much all day. Most days I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling right in my chest. And even when I'm at the clinic, you know, smiling at the patients in Spanish or trying to act normal, I feel like I'm just barely holding myself together. 

I actually learned Spanish in high school. My teacher was Señora Gomez, and she was so intense. She used to make us sing these Spanish pop songs to learn vocabulary. We sang this one song by Shakira, and the music video had her dancing in the mud, which I always thought was so weird. Like, how do you get that out of your hair? I hate getting mud on me. Once when I was little, I fell into a mud puddle in my favorite white dress. My mom was so mad. She tried to bleach it, but it just turned this weird yellowish color. It looked like old teeth. My grandma has dentures, and she takes them out and leaves them in a glass on her nightstand. It used to freak me out when I'd sleep over at her house. I always thought they were gonna chatter at me in the middle of the night like those joke shop wind-up toys. 

But um, yeah. Just empty and heavy, all day. It doesn't really go away.

Um, no, not two years. Like I said, it's just been a year and two months. Two years ago I was sixteen, and things were fine. 

I was actually just learning how to drive back then. My dad taught me in his old Honda Civic, and the air conditioning was completely broken, so we always had to roll the windows down. I got this really fluffy white one from Target, but it got dirty so fast. White is just the worst color for rugs, especially because I always forget to take my shoes off when I come inside. I hate walking barefoot anyway, the floor always feels dusty even if you just swept. Sweeping is the most annoying chore because you can never get that last little line of dirt into the dustpan. He was so mad, he tried to argue with the water police, which is just a terrible idea. He's always arguing with people, though. Just the crackers and coffee mostly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself moving more slowly than usual or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, I guess I have been moving slower. I don't know why they keep the AC cranked up so high in there. We have this one supply tech, Gary, and he wears a winter beanie and a puffy jacket in there in the middle of July. She said it was a fire hazard and threatened to write him up. She takes the fire code very seriously. Like, she made us all practice using the fire extinguishers last month. We had to go out to the back parking lot and spray them at this metal trash can. It was actually kind of fun, the foam goes everywhere. It got all over Dr. Miller's shoes. He wears these really expensive leather loafers, and he was so upset. He spent like twenty minutes in the breakroom trying to wipe them off with paper towels...\n\nBut yeah, I'm just moving really slow lately. Just... just tired and empty, mostly. \n\nThough speaking of edges, the edge of the counter in the breakroom is completely chipped. It's been like that for months, and someone put this bright blue painter's tape over it so nobody would scratch themselves. It looks awful. Brenda keeps saying she's going to put in a work order for maintenance to fix it, but maintenance takes forever. The maintenance guy, Carl, he's always wandering around with this huge ring of keys that jingle so loudly you can hear him coming from down the hall. He has this massive dog, a Great Dane, I think? He brings pictures of it to show everyone. The dog's name is Barnaby, and it sleeps on his couch. He said it actually broke the springs on his old couch because it's so heavy. I can't imagine having a dog that big in an apartment. I used to have a hamster when I was little, named Sir Fluffington. He used to run on his wheel all night long, and it squeaked so much my dad had to take it out of the cage so we could sleep...\n\nBut, um, no. I don't feel anxious. We used to be able to wear any color we wanted, as long as it was solid, but now they're making all the nurses wear this specific shade of navy blue. It's so boring. And the brand they want us to buy from is so expensive. My friend Mark, he's a tech on the fourth floor, he found a loophole and bought this off-brand navy that's like, half a shade lighter, and he hasn't gotten caught yet. He's always trying to find ways around the rules. Like, he parks in the visitor lot instead of the employee garage because it's closer to the entrance, and he just keeps his badge hidden when he walks in. The employee garage is a nightmare anyway. The spaces are so tiny. I drive a really old Honda Civic, and even I have trouble fitting in there. My car's actually been making a weird rattling noise when I go over 40 miles an hour. I think it might be the muffler? My dad said he'd look at it, but he lives two hours away and I hate driving on the highway. The last time I drove on the highway, I saw this huge truck carrying a bunch of logs, and it totally reminded me of that one scary movie where the logs fall off and smash into the car behind it. I was so paranoid the whole time, I stayed like, three lanes away from it...\n\nBut um, yeah. Jogging, knitting, cooking... I just don't care about doing any of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Um, well, like I said, not really. I mean, I used to enjoy a lot of things. Jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... those were my go-to things to clear my head after my nursing classes. But now, it's just nothing. I come home, sit on my bed in my scrubs, and I just can't make myself care about doing any of them. \n\nIt's weird because I used to love reading. I had this huge collection of paperback thrillers. I'd get them from this used bookstore downtown. The smell of that place was amazing, like old paper and dust and coffee. The guy who ran it, Mr. Abernathy, he had this huge orange cat named Marmalade that would just sleep on the stacks of books. Marmalade was so fat, he looked like a pumpkin. My roommate Sarah is actually allergic to cats, which is a bummer because I'd love to get one. But she breaks out in hives if she even looks at a cat. She had to go to the ER once because her throat started closing up after she visited a friend who had a Persian. ERs are so chaotic. I did a clinical rotation in one last semester and it was non-stop. There was this one guy who came in with a fishing hook stuck in his thumb. He was totally calm about it, though. Said he was trying to catch a largemouth bass and the fish fought back. He was wearing this really bright neon green fishing shirt that was practically blinding under the fluorescent hospital lights. Those lights always give me a headache if I stare at them too long. \n\nBut um... yeah. To answer your question, no. I don't really find pleasure in anything right now. Just sitting on my bed in my scrubs.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you find pleasure in, even if it's just a little bit?\n\nPatient: Um, no. Like I've been saying... I really don't. I used to. I used to do a lot of things. Jogging, knitting, cooking, reading... those were my go-to things to clear my head after my nursing classes. But now, it's just nothing. I come home, sit on my bed in my scrubs, and I just can't make myself care about doing any of them. Even just a little bit. \n\nI don't even care about cooking anymore, and I used to make these amazing homemade pizzas. I'd make the dough from scratch and let it rise overnight. The secret is using a really hot pizza stone in the oven. My mom gave me hers when I moved into my apartment. She got it for her wedding back in the nineties. My parents had this huge wedding with like, three hundred guests, which sounds exhausting. They had a live band that played all these cheesy eighties covers. My dad actually got up and sang \"Don't Stop Believin'\" with them. He thinks he's a great singer, but he's totally tone-deaf. He always sings in the shower and it echoes through the whole house. When I was a kid, I used to think there was a ghost in the bathroom making those noises. I was terrified of ghosts. I wouldn't sleep without a nightlight until I was like, twelve. \n\nBut um... no. I don't care about the pizza or the jogging or any of it. Just sitting on my bed.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any periods of feeling slightly better that lasted for at least two months over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. It's just been pretty much the same this whole time. I mean, this has been going on for about a year and two months now. It started right after I graduated high school and started taking my college classes and working full-time at the clinic. \n\nHigh school graduation feels like it was a million years ago, honestly. My grandma flew in from Florida for it, and she brought me this huge bouquet of yellow roses. She loves yellow roses. She grows them in her backyard down there. The weather in Florida is so crazy, though. Like, it'll be perfectly sunny and then suddenly it's pouring rain for ten minutes, and then it's sunny again, but everything is just steaming hot and humid. I don't know how she stands the humidity. It makes my hair frizz up so badly. I tried using this anti-frizz serum my roommate Sarah recommended, but it just made my hair look greasy. Sarah has this perfectly straight, shiny hair, and she doesn't even do anything to it. She just wakes up and it looks great. It's so unfair. She uses this weird coconut shampoo that smells like a tropical vacation, which always makes me crave a piña colada. We actually tried making virgin piña coladas in our blender once, but the blender is super cheap and it didn't crush the ice right, so it was just like... coconut milk with giant chunks of ice floating in it. We ended up just throwing it out. \n\nBut um, anyway. No, I haven't really felt better at all. It's just been this way for a year and two months, since I started all my classes and clinic work.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that you still enjoy or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Um, no... I don't really look forward to anything. Like I said before, I used to do stuff to clear my head after my nursing classes. I'd go jogging, or knit, or cook, or read... but now I just sit on my bed in my scrubs and I can't make myself care about any of it. \n\nI actually bought this really pretty blue yarn a while ago to knit a scarf. It was like a deep navy blue, almost the color of the ocean at night. I went to the beach once at night when I was little, on a family vacation to North Carolina. The sand was so cold, and my brother kept trying to catch these little ghost crabs that were scurrying around. He actually got pinched by one and cried for like an hour. My brother is such a baby when it comes to pain. He got a paper cut last week and acted like he needed stitches. He's studying accounting, which sounds so boring to me. Just looking at spreadsheets all day. I don't know how people sit at desks for eight hours staring at numbers. I need to be moving around, which is why I thought nursing would be good, but now... \n\nNow I just don't want to do anything. Just sit on my bed. So no, nothing I really enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Are you usually sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... pretty much all day. Most days I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling right in my chest. And even when I'm at the clinic, you know, smiling at the patients in Spanish or trying to act normal, I feel like I'm just barely holding myself together. \n\nI actually learned Spanish in high school. My teacher was Señora Gomez, and she was so intense. She used to make us sing these Spanish pop songs to learn vocabulary. We sang this one song by Shakira, and the music video had her dancing in the mud, which I always thought was so weird. Like, how do you get that out of your hair? I hate getting mud on me. Once when I was little, I fell into a mud puddle in my favorite white dress. My mom was so mad. She tried to bleach it, but it just turned this weird yellowish color. It looked like old teeth. My grandma has dentures, and she takes them out and leaves them in a glass on her nightstand. It used to freak me out when I'd sleep over at her house. I always thought they were gonna chatter at me in the middle of the night like those joke shop wind-up toys. \n\nBut um, yeah. Just empty and heavy, all day. It doesn't really go away.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days where you felt completely normal and without any of these heavy, empty feelings over the past year and two months?\n\nPatient: Um, no... I don't think so. I haven't had a normal day since this all started a year and two months ago. It's just been that same heavy feeling every single day. \n\nI actually used to have this calendar on my wall where I'd cross off the days. It had these really cute pictures of golden retriever puppies on it. I love golden retrievers. They just seem so happy all the time, you know? Like they don't have a care in the world, just chasing tennis balls. We had a golden retriever mix when I was growing up named Buster, but he was terrified of thunderstorms. Whenever it rained, he would try to hide under the sofa, but he was way too big, so just his head would be under there and the rest of his body would be sticking out. It was so funny, but also kind of sad. My mom used to try to coax him out with pieces of cheese. She always buys that sharp cheddar cheese with the black wax on the outside. I hate peeling that wax off, it always gets stuck under my fingernails. My nails have been so brittle lately, anyway. I keep breaking them when I'm opening the supply cabinets at the clinic. The cabinets have these awful metal handles that are super sharp on the edges. \n\nBut um, no. No normal days at all. Just the same empty feeling every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt sad or empty for most of every day over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not two years. White is just the worst color for rugs, especially because I always forget to take my shoes off when I come inside.
